# Proposed OEIS comments: A266029 to A270053

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A266029

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A266029>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [1880](../papers/01501-02000/01880-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 11*a(n-1) +231*a(n-2) -100*a(n-3) -7612*a(n-4) -1116*a(n-5) +74582*a(n-6) -64354*a(n-7) -370540*a(n-8) +661109*a(n-9) +1260417*a(n-10) +252581*a(n-11) -15283*a(n-12) +86793*a(n-13) -32631*a(n-14) +2989*a(n-15) -36*a(n-16) for n>17

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 34 931 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 17. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A266063

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A266063>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10695](../papers/10501-11000/10695-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 78 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 97 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 78, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A266072

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A266072>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [180](../papers/00001-00500/00180-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectured g.f.: (1 + x - x^2 + 3*x^3)/(-1 + x^2)^2. - _Michael De Vlieger_, Dec 21 2015
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Dec 21 2015: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct: expanding it and the entry's own definition both give the same linear recurrence, and they agree at enough initial terms for that to settle it. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A266753

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A266753>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [183](../papers/00001-00500/00183-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 20 2016 and Apr 20 2019: (Start)
> Empirical a(n) = (7*4^n - 4)/6 for n>1. - _Colin Barker_, Nov 25 2016 and Apr 20 2019
> a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 2, n>1, conjectured. - _Yosu Yurramendi_, Jan 22 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267638

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267638>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3093](../papers/03001-03500/03093-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +6*a(n-2) -6*a(n-3) -8*a(n-4) +8*a(n-5).
> Empirical: G.f.: -2*x*(1+3*x-x^2-2*x^3+2*x^4) / ( (x-1)*(2*x+1)*(2*x-1)*(2*x^2-1) ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 26 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 40 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267641

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267641>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10555](../papers/10501-11000/10555-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 93 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 2619 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 93, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267867

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267867>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4072](../papers/04001-04500/04072-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 21 2016 and Apr 20 2019: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 4 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268419>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2873](../papers/02501-03000/02873-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) - 20*a(n-2) + 12*a(n-3) + 12*a(n-4) - 8*a(n-5) - 3*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - 6*x + 9*x^2 + 2*x^3 - 4*x^4 - x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - x - x^2)*(1 - 3*x - x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 13 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 44 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268554

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268554>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [10482](../papers/10001-10500/10482-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n^3*(2*n-1)*a(n) -6*(4*n-1)*(3*n-1)*(3*n-2)*(4*n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 10 2016
> For integer r and positive integer s, define sequences {u(n) : n >= 0} and {v(n) : n >= 0} by setting u(n) = [x^(s*n)] F(x)^(r*n) and v(n) = [x^(s*n)] E(x)^(r*n). We conjecture that both u(n) and v(n) satisfy the above supercongruences. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268788

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268788>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10809](../papers/10501-11000/10809-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268892

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268892>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10810](../papers/10501-11000/10810-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269001

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10811](../papers/10501-11000/10811-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269088>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10812](../papers/10501-11000/10812-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
